Can It Last? Keithen Drury, The Motley Fool Wed, April 8, 2026 at 1:13 PM UTC 0 Key Points Nebius and CoreWeave are critical cloud computing businesses. Each is expected to grow its revenue massively over the next few years. 10 stocks we like better than CoreWeave \u203a The \"Magnificent Seven\" cohort of stocks hasn't had the greatest run in 2026. These are the year-to-date stock performances for each company: Nvidia (NASDAQ: NVDA) : -5% Apple (NASDAQ: AAPL) : -6% Alphabet (NASDAQ: GOOG) (NASDAQ: GOOGL) : -3% Microsoft (NASDAQ: MSFT) : -23% Amazon (NASDAQ: AMZN) : -8% Meta Platforms (NASDAQ: META) : -13% Tesla (NASDAQ: TSLA) : -23% Not a single one of those stocks is in the black for the year, making it a fairly low bar to clear when the only expectation is to break even. However, looking at companies that operate in the same industry as these seven is a more interesting comparison. Any artificial intelligence (AI) stocks, in particular, that are outperforming this group catch my eye, as the market has viewed companies in this space through a negative lens. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Two that have outperformed the Magnificent Seven by a wide margin and have produced solid returns so far in 2026 are Nebius (NASDAQ: NBIS) and CoreWeave (NASDAQ: CRWV) . These two are up over 30% and 15%, respectively, for the year and are directly competing against three of the companies in the Magnificent Seven. But can they keep this up? Let's take a look. Upgrade All Stocks ETFs Upgrade --> My Profile Pricing Support Sign out Data Center AI CoreWeave Ends 2 GW Texas Data Center Deal With Poolside April 4, 2026 at 8:14 AM \u2022 by MLQ Agent Key Points CoreWeave and Poolside mutually terminated their agreement for Project Horizon, a planned 2-gigawatt AI data center campus in West Texas.[2][4] The partnership, announced in October 2025, included CoreWeave as anchor tenant for the first 250 MW phase with a 15-year lease and option for 500 MW expansion.[1][4] CoreWeave cited its recent $8.5 billion loan facility as enabling a shift to flexible multi-partner leasing from fixed tenant commitments.[3] Poolside now seeks new partners to advance the project after the deal collapsed.[2][3] Advertisement --> CoreWeave and AI startup Poolside have mutually ended their partnership on Project Horizon, a proposed 2-gigawatt data center in West Texas. The companies cited differing strategic directions and timing issues following CoreWeave's securing of an $8.5 billion loan facility.[1][2][3] Partnership Background CoreWeave and Poolside announced their collaboration in October 2025 to develop Project Horizon, Poolside's planned 2 GW AI campus on 568 acres in West Texas.[1][4] Under the initial agreement, CoreWeave was set to provide more than 40,000 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 GPUs starting in December 2025 and serve as anchor tenant for the first phase of 250 MW capacity under a 15-year lease, with an option for an additional 500 MW.[1][4] The project was structured in eight phases of 250 MW each to support scalable growth for frontier AI training.[4] Reasons for Termination The partnership ended mutually due to diverging strategic paths and timing considerations.[3] CoreWeave's recent $8.5 billion loan facility, backed by a long-term contract with Meta, allowed the company to move away from fixed long-term commitments with single tenants toward a more flexible model involving multiple partners.[3] Poolside, which had planned $2 billion in funding involving Nvidia, now faces a funding gap without a primary tenant.[3] Immediate Next Steps Poolside is actively seeking alternative partners to revive Project Horizon after the collapse of the CoreWeave deal.[2][3] The company emphasized the need for a new anchor tenant to secure financing, as lenders now prioritize projects with long-term contracts from stable companies.[3] Strategic Flexibility Shift CoreWeave's decision reflects a broader shift in the AI infrastructure sector toward capital flexibility. By securing an $8.5 billion loan tied to a Meta contract, CoreWeave gains leverage to avoid high-risk, single-tenant commitments like the 15-year lease for Poolside's 250 MW phase.[3] This move reduces execution risks associated with unproven partners and"

Search Blog Markets Non Sorted Data Processing Servers Apr 6, 2026 CoreWeave AI Cloud Growth Defies Sector Scrutiny in 2025 According to a report from Yahoo Finance, many artificial intelligence stocks have faced recent pressure as investor focus shifts to profitability. Companies not demonstrating solid returns on AI investments are falling out of favor, with unprofitable firms experiencing significant stock declines. CoreWeave , a cloud computing service provider, is highlighted as a company where eventual profitability is considered nearly certain. The firm is built to serve enterprises seeking to utilize or develop AI solutions without constructing their own data centers. Several major technology companies are listed as customers or partners of CoreWeave, which markets itself as an essential cloud for AI. The company's revenue reportedly increased by 110% year-over-year in the final quarter of 2025. Despite this top-line growth, CoreWeave operated at a loss for that quarter and for the full year of 2025. The losses are attributed to significant spending on technology and infrastructure, which represented a major portion of its annual revenue, as the company positions itself for anticipated future AI data center demand expected to emerge over a multi-year period. Analysts anticipate the company will reach profitability by the end of that five-year timeframe. Interactive table based on the Store Companies dataset for this report. Search Blog Computer, Electronic And Optical Products Electronic Components And Boards Multichip Integrated Circuits: Memories Apr 6, 2026 Nvidia and CoreWeave: AI Investment Dynamics and Performance in 2026 According to a recent report from Yahoo Finance, Nvidia and CoreWeave are both significant entities within the artificial intelligence sector. Nvidia holds a leading position in designing AI accelerators, while CoreWeave operates a cloud infrastructure service built for AI applications. Investment Dynamics and Performance The two companies are not market competitors; Nvidia is a notable investor in CoreWeave. However, both stocks attract capital from those looking to gain exposure to the AI industry. Nvidia's financial performance has been strong, with its revenue increasing by 65% in the fiscal year ending in late January. This follows a 78% rise in the prior fiscal period. Over the past three and a half years, its share price has appreciated by approximately 1,360%. The company's market valuation is near $4.1 trillion, and it maintains substantial liquidity and free cash flow. CoreWeave, which became publicly traded in March of last year, has also seen significant growth. Its stock has risen nearly 85% since its debut, albeit with notable price swings. The company reported a 167% annual increase in revenue, surpassing $5.1 billion for the last calendar year. Its contractual backlog reached $67 billion in the final quarter of that year, and its market capitalization stands at about $39 billion. Market Valuation Considerations Nvidia currently trades at a price-to-earnings multiple of 35, which is above the average for the S&P 500 . Given its revenue expansion, some analysts view this valuation as reasonable. The scale of Nvidia's existing market cap, however, presents a challenge for future exponential stock price growth. CoreWeave's smaller market size may offer a different growth profile for investors, supported by its rapidly expanding backlog and revenue. Nvidia and Meta Both Bet on Nebius \u2013 Up 400% in a Year, Is It the Next CoreWeave? Published on: Apr 5, 2026 Author: Sunlight Xiang Share article: Two tech giants are backing the same dark horse in AI cloud infrastructure. Meta signed a $27 billion deal. Nvidia invested $2 billion. And Nebius Group (NBIS) \u2013 up nearly 400% over the past year \u2013 is leaving rival CoreWeave (up 109%) in the dust. With a $25.2 billion market cap, Nebius is still smaller than CoreWeave\u2019s $40.7 billion. But the gap is closing fast. Dual Heavyweight Backing In mid-March, Nebius landed a five-year AI infrastructure agreement with Meta worth up to $27 billion \u2013 $12 billion for dedicated capacity plus a $15 billion option for additional purchases. Around the same time, Nvidia announced a $2 billion strategic stake, helping Nebius deploy over 5 gigawatts of Nvidia systems through 2030. Notably, Nvidia already owned more than 12% of CoreWeave as of late 2025. Betting on both horses says everything about how hot this race is. Scale vs. Speed CoreWeave is still the larger player: $40.7 billion market cap, 43 data centers, 850 megawatts of live power. Nebius had just 170 MW at the end of 2025 but plans to hit 800 MW to 1 GW by end of 2026. But growth tells a different story. Nebius stock: +400% in 12 months CoreWeave stock: +109% in the same period Revenue in Q4: Nebius surged 547% year-over-year to $228 million. CoreWeave grew 110% to $1.57 billion. Both Burning Cash to Grow Neither is profitable yet. Both are spending heavily on GPUs and data centers. Nebius (Q4) CoreWeave (Q4) Net loss $173M $284M Quarterly capex $2B+ $8.2B CoreWeave expects 2026 revenue of $12-13 billion, backed by over $46 billion in contracts with OpenAI and Meta. Nebius holds major deals with Meta ($27B) and Microsoft (up to $19.4B). Risks and the Bigger Picture AI compute rental prices are up 40% since October \u2013 demand remains strong. Nebius recently raised $4 billion in convertible notes and is building a 300 MW data center in Finland, targeting 3 GW of contracted business by 2026. But high capex and fierce competition mean volatility is guaranteed. Nebius offers faster growth, elite customers, and Nvidia\u2019s blessing. CoreWeave still leads in scale and execution. For investors, Nebius is a high-upside bet: if it converts its backlog into profits, the sky\u2019s the limit. If not,"

Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 5, 2026 Coatue Management Exits CoreWeave Stake Ahead of Stock Decline Philippe Laffont's hedge fund, Coatue Management , has sold its entire stake in CoreWeave. The fund's quarterly regulatory filing indicates this position was fully disposed of in the fourth quarter. Coatue had held a stake in CoreWeave since 2024, leading a funding round that year. The position grew following the company's public debut in early 2025 and became the fund's largest holding by the end of the second quarter. The fund began reducing its stake in the third quarter before the complete sale. The timing of the sale preceded a significant decline in CoreWeave's share price, which fell approximately half of its value starting in October. CoreWeave operates in the AI data center sector and utilizes long-term customer contracts to secure financing for expansion, a strategy that results in high leverage relative to its earnings. Investor sentiment shifted after the company reported a delay last fall involving a contractor responsible for building out data center capacity. This event contributed to the stock's decline. Despite this setback, demand for computational resources remains strong, and CoreWeave's primary customers have maintained their relationships with the company. 1. Author Devesh Kumar Apr 08, 2026, 07:56 AM Strong AI demand is driving rapid revenue growth and a massive backlog. Heavy spending and rising debt are pressuring margins and cash flow. Stock remains far below its peak but still above its IPO price. CoreWeave stock (NASDAQ: CRWV) has become one of the more closely watched trades in the AI boom. The company sits in a sweet spot of the market, renting out access to Nvidia-powered computing infrastructure as demand for AI training and inference keeps rising. But the same story that made investors excited is also what is making them nervous: CoreWeave is growing at breakneck speed , spending huge amounts to do it, and still losing money. As of Wednesday, the stock was trading at $85.24, well below its 52-week high of $187.00, though still far above its $40 IPO price from March 2025. Why CoreWeave stock drew so much excitement CoreWeave stock went public in March 2025 and quickly became a high-profile way for investors to bet on the AI infrastructure buildout. Unlike software companies that sell AI tools directly, CoreWeave provides the heavy-duty cloud capacity needed to train and run those models. That has helped it position itself as a pure-play beneficiary of the scramble for high-end GPU access. The growth numbers help explain the early enthusiasm. CoreWeave reported 2025 revenue of $5.1 billion up sharply"

Billionaire Philippe Laffont Sold CoreWeave and Bought This Artificial Intelligence (AI) Stock Instead Motley Fool - Sat Apr 4, 4:20PM CDT Key Points CoreWeave is growing quickly, but it's highly leveraged. Its current share price might look attractive, but it still comes with a lot of risk. Laffont's latest purchase is poised to grow quickly as semiconductor companies step up spending. 10 stocks we like better than CoreWeave \u203a Phillippe Laffont has built a reputation as one of the world's best tech investors. His hedge fund, Coatue Management, is known for making big bets on emerging technology trends. And no trend has had a bigger influence on Coatue's recent portfolio moves than artificial intelligence. Investors can get an idea of what companies Laffont thinks stand to gain the most from mega-trends in technology like artificial intelligence by following Coatue's 13-F filings with the Securities and Exchange Commission. The disclosures show the hedge fund's positions in its publicly traded U.S. stocks each quarter. Coatue's most recent filing shows it sold CoreWeave (NASDAQ: CRWV) , once the fund's largest position, in favor of another great AI stock . Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Here's what investors need to know. Image source: Getty Images. Taking gains on a pre-IPO bet CoreWeave didn't make its public market debut until early 2025, but Coatue has held a stake in the company since 2024. It led the cloud computing company's Series C funding round that year. The hedge fund added to its position following the IPO, and combined with the stock's incredible price performance, it surged to become Coatue's largest holding as of the end of the second quarter. But Laffont and his team started cutting its position in the third quarter before fully disposing of it in the fourth quarter. The timing couldn't have been better. The share price has collapsed since October, down about 50%. CoreWeave is an extremely risky investment. The company specializes in AI data centers. Thanks to a close relationship with Nvidia , a major shareholder, it has access to the high-powered, in-demand graphics processing units (GPUs)"

Nvidia vs. CoreWeave: The Better AI Supercycle Stock Might Surprise You Motley Fool - Sun Apr 5, 2:35AM CDT Key Points Nvidia leads the way in AI, but its size could become an impediment to growth. CoreWeave has made waves with its AI-tailored cloud ecosystem, but it faces a costly build-out to meet demand. 10 stocks we like better than Nvidia \u203a Nvidia (NASDAQ: NVDA) and CoreWeave (NASDAQ: CRWV) often draw comparisons due to their influence over the artificial intelligence (AI) industry. Nvidia remains the dominant design company for AI accelerators, while CoreWeave has attracted business with its cloud infrastructure tailored toward AI. Investors should note that Nvidia and CoreWeave are not competitors. In fact, Nvidia is a major CoreWeave investor. Nonetheless, the two AI stocks compete for investor attention, making an evaluation of which one is a better AI supercycle stock a relevant question to ask. Will AI create the world's first trillionaire? Our team just released a report on the one little-known company, called an \"Indispensable Monopoly\" providing the critical technology Nvidia and Intel both need. Continue \u00bb Image source: The Motley Fool. The case for Nvidia At first glance, Nvidia might be the stock of choice, as companies built the AI supercycle around its AI accelerators. Since just before OpenAI released GPT-4, Nvidia's revenue and stock price have shot into the stratosphere. In fiscal 2026 (ended Jan. 26), its revenue rose by 65%, and this occurred after rising 78% in the previous fiscal year. Despite a slight pullback, Nvidia stock is up by around 1,360% over the last 3.5 years. Additionally, its price-to-earnings (P/E) ratio is 35. In comparison, the S&P 500 's average earnings multiple is 27, and considering Nvidia's revenue growth, it is arguably a cheap stock. Unfortunately, Nvidia may be a victim of its success. Its growth took its market cap to just under $4.1 trillion, making it the world's largest among publicly traded companies. Indeed, 65% revenue growth is impressive considering the difficulty of such an achievement for a large company. Still, that also means a doubling of the stock would take the market cap to $8.2 trillion, a high bar given that Nvidia is the only company that has reached the $5 trillion mark. Such conditions arguably make"

Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 4, 2026 CoreWeave's 2025 Revenue Hits $5.1B Amid $21B Debt and Customer Risks According to a report from Yahoo Finance , CoreWeave, which trades on the NASDAQ , has experienced significant financial expansion. The company's revenue for 2025 reached $5.1 billion, representing a 168 percent increase from the prior year. Management has forecast that revenue will more than double in 2026. Furthermore, the firm's backlog of future business grew by over 300 percent to a total of $66.8 billion. Financial Leverage and Interest Expenses This growth is accompanied by substantial financial obligations. CoreWeave's total debt exceeds $21 billion, a notable increase from under $8 billion in 2024. A large portion of this debt carries a variable interest rate, averaging approximately 11 percent. In the final quarter of 2025, the company's interest expense was $388 million. This quarterly cost means nearly one-third of its revenue is allocated to interest payments. Customer Concentration and Associated Risks The business relies heavily on a small group of major technology and artificial intelligence firms for its revenue. Microsoft and other large-scale cloud providers constitute the majority of its customer base. These entities are considered natural competitors, which could lead them to internalize computing needs in the future. Another key client, OpenAI, is projected to incur substantial losses, including a $14 billion loss for 2026 and potential cumulative losses through 2029. To meet its commitments, OpenAI must secure ongoing large-scale funding, though recent capital raises include significant contingencies and conditions. Potential Economic Headwinds The coming year presents specific challenges. Economic conditions may slow, and geopolitical tensions could elevate the risk of a recession. Concurrently, a potential resurgence in inflation might prompt the Federal Reserve to increase interest rates. Such a move would directly impact CoreWeave's variable-rate debt, potentially raising its borrowing costs further and creating obstacles in securing new financing necessary to convert its backlog into revenue. 1. News Analysts See Over 100% Upside in IREN (IREN) Amid Growing Demand for AI-Linked Compute Infrastructure Published on April 8, 2026 at 10:23 am by Faheem Tahir in News Share IREN Limited (NASDAQ: IREN ) is one of the 7 most oversold data center stocks to invest in . Over 70% of covering analysts, as of April 1, 2026, remain bullish on IREN Ltd. (NASDAQ:IREN). At the same time, the consensus price target of $80 implies an upside of about 130% amid growing demand for AI-linked compute infrastructure. Following IREN Limited\u2019s (NASDAQ:IREN) most recent GPU purchases, H.C. Wainwright reaffirmed its \u201cBuy\u201d rating and $80 price target on March 5, 2026. The investment firm stated that construction at its Childress and Mackenzie data center remains on schedule. It added that IREN Ltd. (NASDAQ:IREN)\u2019s $3.5 billion hardware acquisition, completed before obtaining compute contracts, indicates robust underlying demand. As IREN Limited (NASDAQ:IREN) remains focused on obtaining favorable financing for currently uncontracted hardware, the investment firm identified higher near-term dilution risk amid the company\u2019s plans to potentially sell up to $6 billion in new shares. In the short term, this adds pressure, especially as capital intensity rises throughout the data center industry. According to H.C. Wainwright, IREN Limited (NASDAQ:IREN)\u2019s 2026 outlook turns less risky as a result of these developments, with management\u2019s increased guidance bolstering confidence in expanding its AI Cloud division beyond its previous projections. IREN Ltd (NASDAQ:IREN) is a technology company that operates high-performance, renewable energy-powered data centers specializing in Bitcoin mining and, increasingly, artificial intelligence (AI) cloud services. It provides infrastructure, including GPU-based computing, to support AI, machine learning, and high-performance computing (HPC) for large-scale clients. While we acknowledge the risk and potential of IREN as an investment, our conviction lies in the belief that some AI stocks hold greater promise for delivering higher returns and doing so within a shorter time frame.     "content": "Can IREN's 150K GPU Fleet Drive $3.7B AI Cloud ARR Target in 2026? Search Newsletters Editions Home Latest Business News Money & Markets Tech & Innovation A.I. Lifestyle Leadership \u2709\ufe0f Emails \ud83c\udfa7 Podcasts Untitled Can IREN's 150K GPU Fleet Drive $3.7B AI Cloud ARR Target in 2026? IREN is betting on 150,000 GPUs to reach $3.7B AI cloud ARR by 2026, with $2.3B already under contract and more customer talks underway. Originally published on Zacks Investment Research Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Share to X Share to Facebook Share to Reddit Share to Email Share to Link Add Quartz on Google IREN Limited IREN is increasing its AI cloud capacity to 150,000 graphics processing units (GPUs), which management believes is the key to support its target of more than $3.7 billion in AI cloud annualized run-rate (ARR) revenues by the end of 2026. IREN's latest expansion includes a purchase agreement of more than 50,000 NVIDIA B300 GPUs. This expands the company\u2019s total GPU fleet to 150,000 GPUs. These GPUs are expected to be deployed in Mackenzie, British Columbia and Childress, Texas, using IREN\u2019s existing air-cooled data center capacity. The company plans to deploy the GPUs in phases during the second half of 2026. The target looks possible because IREN already has a good amount of AI cloud revenues under contract. During its fiscal second-quarter 2026 earnings call, management said the company had about $2.3 billion of annualized revenue run rate under contract. This includes the Microsoft $MSFT deal and about $0.4 billion of contracted revenues at Prince George. Management also said customer demand remains strong and that the company is in multiple advanced talks for large-scale deployments. Related Content Southwest raises checked bag fees $10 as airline fuel costs soar Oil plunges toward $90 and the Dow soars 1,300 points after Iran war ceasefire IREN also looks better placed from a funding point of view. The company said it has secured $9.3 billion of funding over the past eight months through customer prepayments, convertible notes, GPU leasing and GPU financing. This matters because AI cloud expansion needs heavy spending upfront. For the latest GPU orders, IREN expects about $3.5 billion of additional capex, including GPUs, servers, storage, networking, labor and other related equipment. For now, the target depends on execution. IREN's ability to deploy these GPUs on time, sign more customer contracts and ramp customer workloads through the second half of 2026 will be the key to hitting the target of $3.7 billion in AI cloud revenues by 2026. The Zacks Consensus Estimate for fiscal 2026 and 2027 indicates revenue growth of around 95% and 193.9%, respectively. IREN Stock Faces Stiff Competition IREN faces intense competition from Applied Digital APLD and TeraWulf WULF in the AI infrastructure space. In January 2026, Applied Digital announced that it had started construction on Delta Forge 1, a large AI data center campus in a southern U.S. state. Delta Forge 1 is designed to"

The comments highlight that the race to expand AI capacity is constrained by real world buildout limits, not just technology or software. For you as an investor, the message is that AI growth is not only a story about GPUs and power contracts. IREN, which sits in the AI and data center ecosystem, is pointing to limits tied to construction timelines, permitting, and on the ground deployment. That context helps frame why some AI infrastructure projects may take longer or carry more execution risk than headlines about chip orders suggest. These remarks may also influence how you think about IREN's long term role and risk profile relative to other AI exposed names. If physical buildout constraints persist, differences in site selection, project management, and capital allocation could matter more for companies like IREN over time than simple capacity targets or theoretical demand projections. IRENs , Strategic IREN's Strategic Pivot Gains Momentum with Texas Facility Launch 08.04.2026 - 00:58:26 | boerse-global.de IREN's 2026 Texas grid connection and $3.4B revenue target face market skepticism over recent losses and a $6B capital raise plan. IREN's Strategic Pivot Gains Momentum with Texas Facility Launch - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de While IREN's shares trade near multi-month lows, the company is on the cusp of a significant operational transformation. The pivotal event is the grid connection of its 1,400-megawatt Sweetwater facility in Texas, scheduled for April 2026. Management has identified this milestone as the primary catalyst for its performance in the first half of the year. Financial Backing and Revenue Roadmap The company's ambitious expansion is underpinned by substantial, secured financing. A cornerstone is a five-year agreement with Microsoft valued at $9.7 billion, supported by $1.9 billion in prepayments. This is complemented by a $3.6 billion GPU financing package secured at an interest rate below six percent. Collectively, these funds cover approximately 95% of IREN's planned GPU investments. As of the end of January, the firm held about $2.8 billion in liquid cash. With the Sweetwater substation transitioning from a cost center to a revenue-generating asset, IREN has outlined a clear deployment schedule. Beginning in mid-2026, the company plans to bring approximately 50 megawatts of data center capacity online each month. The goal is to have 140,000 GPUs delivered and operational by the end of 2026, targeting an annualized revenue run rate of $3.4 billion. Of this projected total, contracts already guarantee $2.3 billion. Market Pressures and Recent Performance Despite these forward-looking plans, the equity has faced headwinds. The market reaction has been tempered by several factors, including new U.S. tariffs on AI chips and the ongoing strategic shift from Bitcoin mining to becoming an AI cloud service provider. Furthermore, IREN announced a potential capital raise of up to $6 billion through an At-The-Market offering, which Co-CEO Daniel Roberts described as a flexible, optional capital tool. This announcement was met with investor skepticism. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying IREN? The company's most recent quarterly results for fiscal Q2 2026 revealed challenges. IREN reported a net loss of $155.4 million, or a loss per share of $0.52, significantly wider than the anticipated loss per share of $0.18. A closer look shows this loss"

IRENs , Strategic IREN's Strategic Pivot to AI Cloud Services Tests Investor Resolve 08.04.2026 - 06:13:57 | boerse-global.de IREN's shift from Bitcoin mining to AI cloud faces investor skepticism over funding and execution, despite a major Microsoft deal and ambitious revenue targets. IREN's Strategic Pivot to AI Cloud Services Tests Investor Resolve - Foto: \u00fcber boerse-global.de The ambitious transformation of IREN from a dedicated Bitcoin mining operation into a provider of artificial intelligence cloud capacity is proving to be a demanding journey for the company and its shareholders. Following a steep decline that erased approximately 20% of its value over just a few trading sessions, the stock demonstrated a notable rebound this Tuesday. This recent volatility underscores concrete investor concerns regarding the financing of its growth plans and the operational complexities involved in overhauling its hardware infrastructure. Wall Street's Divided Verdict on the Strategy Market analysts are offering conflicting assessments of IREN's strategic redirection. This divergence is clearly reflected in the wide range of current price targets: * HC Wainwright maintains a bullish target of $80. * Goldman Sachs sets a more conservative target of $39. * Freedom Capital Markets issues the lowest target among those cited, at $36. Proponents, such as Bernstein, highlight the long-term potential unlocked by the company's partnership with Microsoft and point to the significant capacity of its 4.5-gigawatt portfolio, noting that 90% of this power remains uncontracted. Conversely, skeptics like Freedom Capital caution that the build-out of its AI cloud business may face execution risks and take longer than the market currently anticipates. Should investors sell immediately? Or is it worth buying IREN? Dissecting the Recent Pressure The sell-off that preceded Tuesday's recovery was triggered by a confluence of three key factors. The company's latest quarterly results fell short of expectations, reporting a loss of $0.44 per share. Revenue contracted to just under $185 million, a decline primarily attributed to the costly and complex process of replacing ASIC mining rigs with modern GPU clusters. Furthermore, management's announcement of a potential capital raise aiming to secure up to $6 billion fueled skepticism about possible dilution for existing shareholders. Co-CEO Daniel Roberts defended the plan as a flexible option for funding growth. Adding to the challenging environment, new U.S. tariffs on AI chips have created additional headwinds, weighing on overall investor"

Pre-Market Nebius Group N.V. (NASDAQ: NBIS ) shares are trending on Wednesday. Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland. The new facility will be one of Europe's largest when completed, the company said. In this article NBIS Follow your favorite stocks CREATE FREE ACCOUNT Nebius unveiled plans on Tuesday to build a new AI data center in Finland, which it says will be one of Europe's largest when up-and-running, as the region races to develop the compute infrastructure needed to power the AI boom. The new facility will be based in the Finnish city of Lappeenranta with a capacity of up to 310 MW. Nebius said it expects the data center to begin initially supplying customers by 2027. \"We have been building in Finland for many years and are pleased to be expanding our presence here,\" said Nebius CEO Arkady Volozh in a statement. \"Lappeenranta represents a significant addition to our global AI infrastructure build-out, and will make a significant contribution to achieving our capacity goals.\" Europe's AI buildout It comes amid a slew of AI infrastructure announcements across Europe. French AI startup Mistral said Monday it had secured $830 million in debt financing to operate a data center near Paris. That came after a February announcement of a 1.2-billion-euro ($1.38 billion) plan to build data centers and compute capacity in Sweden. U.K. startup Nscale announced it had raised $2 billion at a $14.6 billion valuation earlier this month, with plans to develop AI data centers in its home market alongside Europe and the U.S. Meanwhile, 2025 saw MGX, Bpifrance, Mistral and Nvidia unveil plans for a 1.4 GW AI campus in France and Brookfield said it"

    "content": "MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra Search Contact sales Log in to Token Factory Log in to AI Cloud AI Cloud Token Factory Solutions Why Nebius Pricing Docs Resources Company For investors Blog / Platform news / MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0: Top-tier AI performance on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra April 1, 2026 14 mins to read Share Today we\u2019re announcing our latest submission to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 benchmark suite, the industry-standard benchmark for measuring AI inference performance developed by MLCommons\u00ae. In this round, Nebius continued to demonstrate strong results for AI inference workloads running on the latest NVIDIA platforms. Our submission includes systems built on NVIDIA Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra GPUs \u2014 NVIDIA HGX B200, NVIDIA HGX B300 and the rack-scale NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 system. We benchmarked three models in this round: DeepSeek R1, Qwen3-VL 235B and gpt-oss 120B. Two of these models, Qwen3-VL 235B, a multimodal model capable of understanding both visual and text inputs; and gpt-oss 120B, a large open-source language model, were introduced to the MLPerf\u00ae Inference benchmark suite in this release. Together with DeepSeek R1, these benchmarks represent the type of frontier-scale models increasingly used in modern AI applications. The MLPerf\u00ae benchmark suite continues to evolve alongside the AI industry, introducing new workloads that reflect the latest trends in large language and multimodal models. Our results in this round demonstrate Nebius\u2019 readiness to run these frontier-scale workloads on cutting-edge AI infrastructure. Ready for frontier model inference Ready for frontier model inference In the MLPerf\u00ae Inference v6.0 round, Nebius benchmarked three NVIDIA AI systems built on the latest Blackwell and Blackwell Ultra architectures: NVIDIA HGX B200 NVIDIA HGX B300 NVIDIA GB300 NVL72 Overall, Nebius achieved 10 first-place results out of 16 benchmark submissions, along with other vendors submitting results. We also submitted five additional use cases where we were the only submitter, so we do not count them as first-place results. Search Blog Markets Insights Apr 6, 2026 Nebius Group Stock Surges 13.8% on Nvidia Investment and Meta Deal According to a report from Yahoo Finance , Nebius Group stock experienced a significant increase during March. The company's shares rose 13.8% for the month. Strategic Investment and Partnerships A key development was an investment in Nebius by Nvidia . This move was seen as a signal of confidence in Nebius's business and its technology across the AI stack. This is notable given that Nvidia also holds a stake exceeding 12% in competitor CoreWeave as of the end of 2025. The investment is expected to support the implementation of over 5 gigawatts of Nvidia systems at Nebius over a five-year span. Separately, Nebius announced a long-term supply agreement with Meta Platforms . This agreement will provide Meta with AI infrastructure capacity valued at $12 billion, with deployment from various locations beginning early next year. The deal also includes an additional commitment from Meta to purchase $15 billion in available compute capacity over five years. Expansion and Market Conditions Nebius is continuing to invest in expanding its capacity. The company raised more than $4 billion last month through a convertible note offering, with proceeds earmarked for cloud capacity growth. This includes a new data center project in Finland announced at the end of March, designed with a capacity exceeding 300 megawatts. High demand for AI compute is influencing the market. One industry newsletter reported a 40% rise in some AI infrastructure rental prices since October. Nebius also intends to continue serving spot market customers alongside its long-term agreements. 1. For Nebius Group, the model used is a 2 Stage Free Cash Flow to Equity approach. The latest twelve month free cash flow is a loss of about $3,545.57 million. Analysts provide free cash flow estimates through 2030, with projections in this period moving from losses into a forecast of $4,780 million of free cash flow in 2030. Beyond the analyst horizon, Simply Wall St extends the cash flow path using its own assumptions for several more years. Aggregating and discounting these projected cash flows gives an estimated intrinsic value of about $606.89 per share. Compared with the recent share price around $112.54, the DCF output points to an implied discount of roughly 81.5%. Search Blog Computer, Electronic And Optical Products Electronic Components And Boards Multichip Integrated Circuits: Memories Apr 6, 2026 AI Infrastructure Stocks: Broadcom, Nvidia, Nebius Lead 2030 Data Center Buildout According to a report from Yahoo Finance, artificial intelligence represents a significant long-term investment opportunity with broad economic implications. The analysis suggests that public investors might consider companies supplying computing components, as these firms stand to benefit from the AI expansion irrespective of which specific AI developers ultimately succeed. The report highlights three stocks as prominent choices in the current AI infrastructure buildout: Broadcom , Nvidia , and Nebius . These companies are noted for having substantial and expanding revenue streams. Broadcom and Nvidia are identified as competitors within the same market segment, though the scale of the opportunity is considered sufficient for multiple participants. Industry projections indicate data center spending will reach very high levels by 2030. Both Broadcom and Nvidia are positioned to secure a significant share of the computing power demand within this trend. Nvidia's graphics processing units are described as the established standard for AI computing, a leadership position viewed as highly secure. Broadcom, in contrast, pursues a different strategy by collaborating with clients to design custom AI chips tailored for specific workloads. This approach can yield performance and cost advantages for certain applications, and such custom chips are seen as potentially complementary to Nvidia's general-purpose GPUs. The analysis concludes that both companies are poised for considerable growth in the coming period, a prospect that may not be fully reflected in their current market valuations. Search Blog Markets Insights Array Nebius AI Computing Expansion: From 2 to 16 Data Centers by 2026 Loading Stock video by CreativeWorlds99 via Pixabay Apr 4, 2026 Nebius AI Computing Expansion: From 2 to 16 Data Centers by 2026 According to a report from Yahoo Finance, Nebius , a company operating in the neocloud and AI computing sector, is positioned for significant growth. The firm has secured a strategic partnership with Nvidia , which has invested billions of dollars and provides early access to its newest computing equipment. This relationship has helped attract major corporate clients, including Meta Platforms and Microsoft , alongside individual developers. Infrastructure and Financial Growth The company has rapidly increased its data center footprint, growing from two sites in 2024 to seven by the close of 2025. Plans indicate an expansion to 16 sites by the end of 2026. This scaling is reflected in its financial performance, with the core AI business reporting a substantial year-over-year revenue increase in the final quarter of 2025. The company anticipates its annual revenue run rate will rise significantly from its level at the end of 2025. This expansion is driven by demand from large technology companies seeking rentable computing capacity. If Nebius can maintain this pace of growth and secure long-term client relationships, it has the potential to become a major entity in the technology industry. 1. And the company is planning to spend $16 billion to $20 billion on data centers in 2026 alone. The question for investors is no longer whether Nebius can grow. The question is whether the growth justifies the valuation, whether the capital structure can sustain the buildout, and whether this stock has more in common with early Amazon Web Services or with the debt-fueled infrastructure bets that imploded in the early 2000s. From Yandex\u2019s Ashes to AI Neocloud Understanding Nebius requires understanding where it came from. By Sarah Motter and Chandler Watkins Published : Mar. 25, 2026 at 4:57 PM CDT Comment 0 Share Add Us On Google Add as a preferred source on Google INDEPENDENCE, Mo. (KCTV) - A Jackson County judge has blocked residents from voting on a massive AI data center tax deal. Court records show that on Wednesday, March 25, Judge Jennifer M. Phillips denied the declaratory judgment and injunctive relief petition filed by three Independence residents. The residents sought to force a citywide vote on the tax incentive package tied to the Nebius data center project. ALSO READ: Residents continuing to fight after judge blocks referendum deadlines What the judge decided The court says it found that the ordinance authorizing the city to issue up to $150.6 billion in revenue bonds for the project went into effect immediately under the city charter. The provision covers ordinances related to specific contracts and bond issuances, placing them outside the reach of a public referendum. In plain terms: because the ordinance took effect the moment the council passed it on March 2 , residents had no legal window to trigger a referendum under the charter. Residents oppose the Nebius data center on March 19, 2025. (KCTV5/Zach Fisher) According to the judgment, the court\u2019s previous temporary injunction, issued on March 18 , was also quashed on Wednesday. Join the Conversation 0 0 comments The City of"

Stocks Best Quality Stocks Best Value Stocks Best Growth Stocks Recent Markets April 2, 2026 4:23 PM 2 min read Nebius Group Stock Jumps Thursday: What Investors Need To Know Nebius Group stock is charging ahead with explosive momentum. What\u2019s fueling NBIS momentum? Finland AI Data Center Plan Adds Scale To Expansion Story NBIS fell to about $90 late Monday before climbing to over $108 by Thursday's close, a rebound of roughly 21% in just a few sessions. That kind of move suggests traders may be refocusing on the company's recent execution and expansion updates rather than the early-week weakness. One key catalyst came Tuesday, when Nebius said it plans to build a 310-megawatt AI data center in Lappeenranta, Finland, with initial capacity expected in 2027."
